The interval is deliberately wider than RAR-P20's individual CIs because the thing being tested is precisely whether compounding across a drifting session was legitimate. | **This row is a consistency check on RAR-P20 as much as a new number.** A result inside the band says the two hops were internally sound despite the drift, and A.4.4 may quote the direct figure. A result **outside +5.0% to +9.5%** says compounding was not legitimate, and the reading is not "the ladder changed" but "one of RAR-P20's runs carried something the interleaving did not absorb" - in which case RUN2 and RUN3 are re-examined before any README text is written, and the individual hops are re-measured rather than patched. **RESULT - RUN 2026-09-10: +6.65%, 95% CI [+6.48%, +6.89%], inside the frozen band.** Pooled median 2,963,438 -> 3,160,590 n/s; pooled best-of +7.40%; per-build medians 2.955-2.969 M against 3.153-3.167 M, with no overlap between the arms. Under the reading rule the first branch fires: the two RAR-P20 hops were internally sound, and A.4.4 may quote **+6.65%** as the measured cost of running `base` on a CPU that could run `pext`. | **Calibration, appended after exposure; the prediction is not rewritten.** Sign correct, and the result landed inside the frozen +6.0% to +8.5% band - but **below** the +7.2% point estimate, and that gap is the finding. **Chaining overstated the ladder by 0.50 percentage points, and the direct interval excludes the chained value**: compounding RAR-P20's hops gives 1.0459 x 1.0245 = **+7.15%**, which sits outside [+6.48%, +6.89%]. So the decision not to let A.4.4 quote the chained figure was correct on the merits, not merely cautious. **What was actually wrong is identifiable, and it is not the drift.** The suspicion on record was that the +1.46% session drift between RUN2 and RUN3 had contaminated the compounding. It had not - interleaving absorbed it, as designed. The culprit is **precision**: RUN2's interval was [+3.79%, +5.83%], **2.04 pp wide**, so its +4.59% point estimate carried about a percentage point of slack. Back out the direct result through the well-measured `pext`-over-`avx2` hop and `base`-to-`avx2` implies **+4.10%** - comfortably inside RUN2's own CI, so nothing contradicts anything. The chained number was not wrong so much as it inherited RUN2's imprecision and then multiplied it. **The direct run is 5x tighter at 0.41 pp**, which is what a single interleaved comparison buys over two composed ones. **Transferable rule: compose ratios only when each is tighter than the difference you care about.** Two hops at +-1 pp cannot answer a question to +-0.5 pp, however carefully each hop was run. **Drift continued and remained harmless:** `base` read 2,922-2,934k in RUN1, 2,940-2,961k in RUN2 and 2,955-2,969k here, an upward march across the session consistent with thermal settling, and absorbed within each run exactly as the alternating-direction design intends. **Speed is not Elo.** At RAR-M20's ~2 Elo per 1% this is roughly **13 Elo** between the extreme assets - a real number for a user who picks wrong, and still a conversion for reasoning about candidates rather than a gate result. **RAR-P20 is not superseded**: its two hops stand as measured, and this row refines the composite they could not deliver. **Speed is not Elo.** At RAR-M20's ~2 Elo per 1% the result would imply roughly 12 to 17 Elo between the extreme assets, and that conversion is for reasoning about candidates, never a gate. **No decision rule is attached because none is owed:** this measurement selects nothing and accepts nothing. It exists so that a number the README will print to users is measured rather than inferred. **Retry trigger:** re-run when the tier set changes, when NNUE lands in Phase F, or on a pinned-compiler bump. | PLAN A.4.3, A.4.4; RAR-P20 (the two hops and the drift finding); RAR-M20; `tools/results/tier-nps-20260910/` |
| RAR-P21 | **A.4.1 `cc` 1.3.0 -> 1.4.5 and the Fathom build contract, COMPLETE 2026-09-10 (`1bf8171`).** `cc` compiles `vendor/fathom/src/tbprobe.c`, and `build.rs` hands it a tier-dependent `TB_NO_HW_POP_COUNT` derived from `CARGO_CFG_TARGET_FEATURE`; mis-handling that define is how the 2.3.0 and 2.3.1 baseline assets shipped **15 illegal `popcntq`**, so a `cc` bump is a build-contract change and not a version number. Manifest floor raised `cc = "1.3"` -> `"1.4"` so it tracks what has been verified rather than what merely resolves; `find-msvc-tools` follows 0.1.9 -> 0.1.12. The bump also exposed an **A.3.1 oversight**: `Cargo.toml` asserted in a comment that MSRV is kept in lockstep with `rust-toolchain.toml`, but the toolchain moved to 1.98.1 while `rust-version` stayed at `1.97` - visible because cargo reported "Locking 2 packages to latest Rust 1.97 compatible versions". Corrected to `1.98`; it changed nothing here (1.4.5 is latest under either floor) but the asserted invariant was false. Hosts: 5950X Windows x86-64 and the MacBook Air M4, both on `rustc 1.98.1`. | **GREEN on every checked surface, and one prior claim of mine is corrected below.** `verify-isa` holds on `base`, `avx2`, `pext` and `pext --pgo` on Windows x86-64 and on `arm64 --pgo` on macOS; **base is `popcnt 0`, `bmi2 0`**, so the 2.3.0-era defect has not returned. Bench fingerprint **7,601,220 / EBF 2.474** on all three x86 tiers. PGO builds, profile merges. Tests: debug **283** passed, release **284**, **0 failed**, 26/26 targets `ok` in each; the single release-only test is `#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]` by construction; `xtask` and `texel-tuner` 15 passed. `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` **zero**. **The macOS question is answered, and the answer is not the one the question assumed.** `cc` 1.4.5 does **not** close the gap - the M4 still prints `Inherited flag "-fprofile-use=..." is not supported by the currently used CC` - so it is not a `cc` defect. Chasing it further produced the real finding: **no platform compiles Fathom with PGO.** The Windows C invocation, captured in full, is `cl.exe -nologo -MD -O2 -Brepro -I vendor/fathom/src -W0 /TP /std:c++17 -DTB_NO_HELPER_API -Fo... -c tbprobe.c` - **no profile flag of any form**. macOS is simply the only platform that says so out loud, because there the compiler is clang-like, `cc` tries to translate `-C profile-use` into `-fprofile-use`, and the support probe rejects it; MSVC has no LLVM-profile equivalent at all, so the flag is dropped without comment. (The same command also confirms the tier logic: `TB_NO_HW_POP_COUNT` is correctly **absent** on `pext`, whose baseline includes POPCNT.) | **CORRECTION to an earlier claim in this project's own notes, recorded rather than quietly fixed.** RAR-P19 and the first draft of PLAN A.4.1 said Windows "shows no such warning", with the implication that the C half is profiled there and macOS is the outlier. That was wrong, and it was wrong in the standard way: *absence of a warning was read as presence of a behaviour.* **Two checks I ran failed as instruments before one worked**, which is the transferable lesson. `CC_ENABLE_DEBUG_OUTPUT=1` through `xtask` returned zero hits for `fprofile-use` - but a positive control for flags that MUST be present (`TB_NO_HELPER_API`, `/TP`, `std:c++17`) also returned **zero**, proving the log never contained C flags and the null was empty. A `cargo build -vv` run then failed its positive control too, until `CC_ENABLE_DEBUG_OUTPUT=1` and `-vv` were combined, at which point the controls read 1, 4 and 2 and the invocation appeared. **Never accept a null from a log until a flag you know is present shows up in it.** Practical consequence of the finding: negligible and uniform. **Tablebases are unaffected and work normally** - this is about how `tbprobe.c` is compiled (`-O2`, no profile guidance), not about whether probing runs or is correct. The only party affected is a user who configures `SyzygyPath`, who gets correct probing that is marginally less optimised. It cannot have contaminated any measurement: `SyzygyPath` defaults to empty and `can_probe` returns false while `largest() == 0`, `sprt.ps1` and `gauntlet.ps1` never set it, and `datagen.ps1` passes `-tb`/`-tbpieces`/`-tbadjudicate` to **fastchess**, so even there the adjudicator's tablebase code is used and not Rarog's Fathom. **Not worth fixing:** MSVC PGO (`/GL` + `/LTCG:PGI`) cannot consume rustc's LLVM profile, so there is no shared-profile route on the primary platform. **Retry trigger:** revisit only if D.4 makes probe throughput matter, or if the C surface grows beyond one file. **Still open, by design not by omission:** Windows ARM64 has not rebuilt under `cc` 1.4.5; the A.9 release CI covers it, since RAR-P15 established that its bench cells run `verify-isa` on all five platform cells and check cross-platform determinism. | PLAN A.4.1; RAR-P18 (the MSRV oversight's origin); RAR-P19 (the warning this row re-explains); RAR-P15 (what the CI matrix covers); `build.rs`; `Cargo.toml`; `Cargo.lock` |
| RAR-P20 | **A.4.1 per-tier NPS: what does the ISA tier actually buy? REGISTERED 2026-09-10, NOT YET RUN.** The A.4 design choice between a fat binary (option a) and multiversioning only the PEXT kernels (option b) turns on one number the ledger does not contain: what **global** `x86-64-v3` codegen is worth outside the slider kernels. Option (b) keeps one ~800 KB binary but compiles everything except the multiversioned kernels at baseline; if that costs nothing, (a) buys ~2.4x binary size for nothing, and if it costs several percent, (b) is not viable. A second question rides along: whether the `pext` tier earns its place over `avx2` at all. **Conditions:** idle 5950X (maintainer confirmed idle 2026-09-10), Windows x86-64, `rustc 1.98.1`, source `ef9c6ae`, clean tree. **Four independent PGO builds per tier** via `tools/nps_build_pool.ps1`, which refuses the pool unless every copy is SHA-256-distinct (identical bytes mean the profile did not vary and pooling measures nothing) and every copy reproduces bench fingerprint **7,601,220** before entering the pool. Measured with the existing `tools/nps_multibuild.ps1 -Cycles 10 -Repeats 3`, which interleaves one reading of every build per cycle, alternates direction, and reports a pooled-median delta with a 2,000-sample bootstrap CI. **Three runs, in this order:** (1) **NULL**, `base{1,2}` against `base{3,4}` - same source, same tier, different profile luck; (2) **avx2 against base** - the deciding number, no source difference, codegen only; (3) **pext against avx2** - the `rarog_pext` source path in `attacks.rs`. | **Prediction, frozen 2026-09-10 before any run.** NULL: straddles zero, absolute pooled median **under 1%**. avx2 vs base: **+2% to +6%** - Rarog is a bitboard engine and `x86-64-v3` brings BMI1 (`andn`, `blsr`, `tzcnt`) and LZCNT into every move loop, not only the sliders. pext vs avx2: **+2% to +7%** on fast-PEXT hardware. Confidence moderate on the signs, low on the magnitudes; the honest state is that neither has ever been measured in this engine. **PROSPECTIVE DECISION RULE, fixed now so it cannot be invented after exposure.** (i) If the NULL fails to straddle zero or its absolute median reaches 1%, the instrument is not calibrated at this resolution and **runs 2 and 3 are not read at all** - the tier question stays open and the pool size goes up. (ii) If run 2's CI **upper** bound is below **+1.5%**, global v3 codegen is worth under about 3 Elo, option (b) becomes the preferred design and A.4.2 is re-scoped to multiversioning. (iii) If run 2's CI **lower** bound is above **+1.5%**, option (a) is confirmed and A.4.2 proceeds as the fat binary. (iv) If the CI spans +1.5%, the run is **inconclusive**: default to (a), record it as unresolved rather than as support. (v) If run 3's CI straddles zero, the `pext` tier does not pay for itself and the universal binary ships **two** tiers, not three. The **1.5%** threshold is not free-chosen: RAR-M20 records the ~2 Elo per 1% NPS constant at STC, so 1.5% is about 3 Elo, the smallest tier difference worth carrying a second code path for. **RESULT - RUN 2026-09-10, all three runs complete; option (a) confirmed and all three tiers earn their place.** Source `fc6e39d`, engine-identical to `ef9c6ae` (only `EXPERIMENTS.md`, `GUIDE.md`, `PLAN.md` and `tools/nps_build_pool.ps1` differ, zero paths under `src`, `xtask`, `build.rs` or `Cargo.*`); twelve builds, all SHA-256-distinct, all at fingerprint 7,601,220. **RUN1 NULL** `base{1,2}` against `base{3,4}`: **-0.15%**, 95% CI **[-1.26%, +0.66%]** - straddles zero with an absolute median under 1%, so clause (i) passes and runs 2 and 3 are readable. **RUN2 avx2 against base**: **+4.59%**, CI **[+3.79%, +5.83%]**, per-build medians 2.94-2.96 M against 3.08-3.09 M with every candidate build above every baseline build. **RUN3 pext against avx2**: **+2.45%**, CI **[+2.32%, +2.62%]**, again with no overlap between the two arms' per-build medians. **Verdict under the frozen rule:** clause (iii) fires - RUN2's lower bound of +3.79% is two and a half times the +1.5% threshold - so **option (a), the fat binary, is confirmed and option (b) is rejected**, because multiversioning only the slider kernels would forfeit the 4.6% that global `x86-64-v3` codegen delivers everywhere else in the engine. Clause (v) does not fire: RUN3's interval excludes zero by a wide margin, so a universal binary carries **three** tiers, not two. | **Calibration, appended after exposure; the prediction above is not rewritten.** Both signs and both magnitudes landed inside the frozen bands - avx2 against base predicted +2% to +6% and measured **+4.59%**; pext against avx2 predicted +2% to +7% and measured **+2.45%**. The one soft miss is **magnitude on RUN3**: the result sits at the very bottom of a band whose width admitted +7%, so that band was too generous rather than wrong, and a future prediction about swapping a single source path should be narrower. **A CONFOUND worth more than the calibration: the host drifted upward across the session, and the same binaries prove it.** The four `avx2` builds pooled to **3,087,416** n/s as the candidate arm in RUN2 and to **3,132,586** n/s as the baseline arm in RUN3 minutes later - **+1.46% for identical bytes**. `base` moved the same way, 2,929,182 in RUN1 to 2,951,930 in RUN2, **+0.78%**. The likeliest cause is thermal and boost settling after the twelve-build burst that immediately preceded RUN1. **This touches none of the three verdicts**, because `nps_multibuild.ps1` interleaves both arms within a run and alternates direction, so a slow drift loads both arms equally - that is what the design is for. What it forbids is **chaining runs**: the tempting base-to-pext figure of about **+7.2%** (4.59% compounded with 2.45%) spans the drift and is an **estimate, not a measurement**. That is also the number that says what a user on the wrong asset actually loses, so if it is going to be quoted it needs its own direct `pext` against `base` run, about ten minutes. **Appended 2026-09-10: that run is RAR-P22, and it measured +6.65% [+6.48%, +6.89%], so the ~7.2% chained figure above is superseded for quotation - the direct interval excludes it. The cause was RUN2's 2.04 pp width, not the drift.** **Speed is still not Elo.** At RAR-M20's ~2 Elo per 1% constant the ladder is worth roughly 9 Elo from base to avx2 and about 5 more to pext, but that constant is for reasoning about candidates and no gate has measured it in this engine. **Retry trigger** unchanged: a change to the tier set, NNUE landing in Phase F (which is what would make AVX-512 tiers worth revisiting), or a pinned-compiler bump. **Leaf identifiers:** the prototype leaf named in clauses (ii) and (iii) was renumbered A.4.2 to A.4.3 on 2026-09-10 when the `cc`/Fathom leaf was inserted ahead of it; bounds, thresholds and rule text are unchanged. **Read this row for what it is: a zero-game speed measurement, and speed is not Elo.** RAR-M20's constant is a conversion for reasoning about candidates, never a substitute for a gate. **Cost is about 40 minutes** - twelve PGO builds at roughly 45 s, then three pooled runs of about 10 minutes. And **the engine ignores `argv`**: `main` goes straight to the UCI loop, so `rarog.exe bench 13` prints the banner and exits at EOF having benched nothing, while an explicit `quit` on stdin kills a bench already dispatched to the engine thread. Both builds fingerprint **6,502,902**, matching x86 exactly, so the hint is behaviour-neutral as it must be. The ISA contract behaved as its own negative control: the baseline asset FAILED with `REQUIRED prefetch never appears` and the candidate passed. Topology: `hw.cachelinesize` **128**, `hw.pagesize` **16384**, L1d 64 KB, L2 4 MB. | **Two lessons.** (1) **The 128-byte Apple cache line is real, but `3ee4660` aimed at the wrong hazard.** Neither cluster type can straddle a 128 B line (32 and 64 both divide 128, both self-aligned), so the alignment wrapper addresses something that cannot happen. The REAL exposure is that `SharedCluster` is `align(64)` and documented as 'exactly one cache line' — true on x86-64, FALSE on Apple Silicon, where two independent clusters share a line and two threads can contend over unrelated entries. That is a Threads>1 ARM64 question and needs a 4T ARM A/B before anything is over-aligned, since naive padding would halve TT density. (2) **PGO does not reach the vendored C on macOS:** `cc` rejects the inherited `-fprofile-use`, so Fathom builds unoptimised there — the same 'the build contract reaches the Rust half only' shape as RAR-P09's popcnt finding, low impact because it is tablebase-probe code, but it is a gap in the release pipeline rather than a quirk. | `src/tt.rs`; Plan 4.8b, 4.8c |
| RAR-P10 | Phase-4.8b: port the AArch64 TT prefetch from `origin/arm_fix` onto current development and make its presence enforceable. Emission proved by compiling the exact `prefetch_ptr` body standalone for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` at `-O` (the vendored Fathom C build blocks a full cargo cross-compile on this host). | **ACCEPTED on measurement: +1.42% NPS on an M4, and a silent three-release loss closed.** `prefetch_ptr` had an x86 body and `let _ = ptr;` for everything else, so **all three shipped ARM64 assets did no TT prefetching at all** while the x86 assets did. `prfm pldl1keep` (the ARM analogue of `_MM_HINT_T0`) now compiles in behind an exact `target_arch` cfg; the probe emits it at every call site and it survives `#[inline(always)]` at `-O`. `cargo xtask verify-isa --arch arm64` now REQUIRES the class, so every CI bench cell and every release asset proves it is present. x86-64 is untouched: bench 6,502,902 / EBF 2.449, all three x86 tiers still pass their contracts. | **A missing cache hint is invisible to every instrument this project owns.** The engine plays identically with and without a prefetch - same nodes, same moves, same fingerprint, same tests - it merely plays slower, so node agreement across CI cells cannot see it and neither can a strength gate on x86. A REQUIRED instruction class is the only check that catches it, which generalises: for a hint-shaped optimisation, verify the instruction, not the behaviour. The A/B PLAN 4.8 item 3 requires has now run (RAR-P11) and the port is KEPT. The sibling `origin/arm_fix` commit (`3ee4660`, Apple TT cache-line alignment) is deliberately NOT ported - it has no ARM timing evidence and PLAN 4.8 item 4 requires an Apple result first. | `src/tt.rs`; `xtask/src/main.rs`; Plan 4.8b |
| RAR-P09 | Phase-4.8a: freeze the per-tier ISA contract and make it EXECUTE. Disassembled every x86-64 asset with `llvm-objdump` and classified every mnemonic; added `cargo xtask verify-isa`, which asks rustc what the tier's `target-cpu` enables and holds the artifact to it. Windows x86-64, pinned rustc 1.97.1. | **Two shipped defects found, both invisible to every existing gate.** (1) The `x86-64` baseline asset contained **15 `popcntq`**, every one from `vendor/fathom`: `-C target-cpu` is a rustc flag that `cc` never sees and Fathom selected hardware popcount without a feature test. Fixed by defining `TB_NO_HW_POP_COUNT` when the target lacks the feature. (2) The startup CPU guard **could never fire in a specialized asset** because the statically required feature made detection fold to true. The dead guard was removed and README states the measured requirement per asset. | **Three transferable lessons.** (a) An ISA tier is only as strong as its weakest translation unit. (b) A runtime check for a statically-required feature is true by construction; keep specialized binaries simple and consider only a complete baseline universal dispatcher in Phase 8.1. (c) Ask the compiler, not folklore: derive contracts from `rustc --print cfg`. | `build.rs`; `src/main.rs`; `xtask/src/main.rs`; `README.md`; `.github/workflows/`; `PLAN.md` Phase 8.1 |

## 7. Correctness and protocol lessons

| ID | Experiment or failure mode | Disposition | Conditional lesson / coverage | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RAR-C01 | Self-consistency tests compared implementations sharing the same rule-50, repetition and SEE omissions. | Independent legal-exchange and external perft oracles added/required. | Green correlated tests do not establish correctness. Important invariants need an implementation-independent oracle. | legacy plan; `analysis/infra_analysis.md` |
| RAR-C02 | Rule-50 draw could override mate; null moves changed the halfmove clock; repetition lacked root/null awareness. | Free mate-precedence fix retained; optional draw-policy bundle followed RAR-S18's strength verdict. | Separate legal terminal precedence from heuristic repetition policy; they can have different acceptance criteria. | legacy plan; `analysis/search_analysis.md` |
| RAR-C03 | Multi-thread diagnostics reset/dumped inside helper-called root search and the diagnostic build had stopped compiling. | Fixed; previous multi-thread counter history was declared unreliable. | Telemetry must have one owner and a build/runtime canary before it can guide search decisions. | `CHANGELOG.md` 2.3.1; legacy plan |
| RAR-C04 | Aspiration mate-score re-search could fail to terminate; capture cutoffs could train quiet correction. | Fixed and regression-covered. | Rare control-flow and attribution faults can contaminate root/time/history together. Retain deterministic tests before strength gates. | `CHANGELOG.md` 2.3.1 |

## 8. Cross-engine evidence imported from Basilisk

These are ideas, warnings or ordering priors already incorporated where useful
in Rarog's forward plan. No additional roadmap item is created merely by
listing them here.

| ID | Basilisk evidence | Possible Rarog implication | Existing PLAN coverage |
|---|---|---|---|
| RAR-X01 | TT-bound-aware pruning evaluation gained +7.18 Elo while preserving raw/corrected eval for learning. | Strong prior for typed result evidence and producer/consumer capability separation, not for copying its TT layout. | 4.6 |
| RAR-X02 | Check-extension removal lost −10.17 ± 6.52 in Basilisk while Rarog's extension had gained +30.75. | Confirms that extensions and their LMR/pruning consumers co-adapt. Rework only inside prospective-depth/refit gates. | 4.7, 4.8 |
| RAR-X03 | Stockfish distillation gained +6.75 in Basilisk but lost −17.11 in Rarog. | Teacher/corpus/representation fit dominates transfer; a sibling success cannot reopen RAR-E03 unchanged. | 4.12–4.16 for evaluator contracts; 5.0, 6.0–6.2, 7.0–7.2 for teacher/data |
| RAR-X04 | A 6-ply continuation-history channel lost −7.70 in Basilisk. | Wider history distance can duplicate existing signals. Rarog should prove unique held-out attribution before adding contexts. | 4.5 |
| RAR-X05 | Exact/PV reward-only history and surprise scaling jointly reverified at +3.06 ± 4.35. | Result kind and confidence can be useful training inputs when sibling maluses are not misapplied. | 4.5, 4.6 |
| RAR-X06 | Root instability TM reverified at +6.46 ± 4.12, while Rarog's raw pool-view variant lost −5.54. | Instability may help only when derived from a completed authoritative root snapshot and bounded with other confidence signals. | 4.12a (retargeted from 4.9, which is now HCE structure) |
| RAR-X07 | Basilisk's +4.34% NPS wave measured +8.69 ± 6.63 Elo at STC; some cached-check/pin optimizations that helped Rarog were negative there. | Speed-to-Elo direction is corroborated near this TC, but individual hot-path optimizations are profile- and language-specific. | 4.19, 8.1 |
| RAR-X08 | Basilisk's `arm_fix` independently tried unmeasured TT over-alignment; its shipped build has clearer ISA-tier documentation but also a PEXT documentation/flag mismatch. | Corroborates the need to measure topology and audit the executable asset contract, not the proposed wrapper. | 4.19, 8.1 |
| RAR-X09 | Basilisk's SMP safety bundle gained +30.42 ± 8.77 at 4T, smaller than Rarog's five-change +102.78 bundle. | The different gains suggest different baseline defects; compare ownership/clock/TT interactions without assigning Elo to individual components. | 4.19, 8.0 |

The cross-review found no additional high-value Basilisk item missing from the
current Rarog plan. Items above are already covered, contradicted by local
evidence, or deliberately postponed to the NNUE/scaling phases.

Anything not meeting its trigger stays closed. A retry is a new experiment with
a new ID and manifest; it does not overwrite the historical row.

## 10. Template for a new experiment

```markdown
### RAR-<area><number> — <short name>

- Date / owner:
- Baseline SHA / candidate SHA / dirty-diff hash:
- Binary / compiler / PGO identity:
- Research question:
- Hypothesis / proposed mechanism:
- Competing hypotheses:
- Interacting mechanisms / consumers:
- **PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION (freeze before exposure):**
  - Expected diagnostic movement:
  - Expected Elo sign/range, if defensible:
  - Probability positive/useful and confidence basis:
  - Most likely failure mode:
- Falsification criteria:
- Cheapest prior falsifier: test / result / implementation still justified?:
- Registered gate and stop rule:
- Full conditions / provenance: flags, manifests and hashes; book/hash, TC,
  threads, Hash, concurrency, affinity, adjudication, node budget and cohort:
- Result:
  - Diagnostics: nodes, EBF, NPS, depth, counters, suites (not the verdict):
  - Games/verdict: games, W-D-L, Elo/nElo and CI, LLR:
- Disposition: accepted / retained / rejected / neutral/inconclusive /
  observation / no-change / deferred:
- **PREDICTION CALIBRATION (append after exposure):**
  - Original prediction (do not rewrite):
  - Observed result; sign and magnitude reasonable?:
  - Proposed causal mechanism supported?:
  - Missed interaction or instrument failure?:
  - Confidence over/under-calibrated?:
- Postmortem: changed causal assumption / what did not change / alternatives:
- Conditional lesson:
- Retry trigger or `closed`:
- Artifacts / commits:
```
